610. What is the tongue sense base? … by which the tongue … is pushed against in taste … that is the tongue sense base. ^1-610

611. What is the tongue sense base? … depending on which tongue, based on taste, tongue-contact arose … depending on which tongue, with taste as object, tongue-contact arose … feeling … perception … intention … tongue-consciousness arose — that tongue, that tongue sense base, that tongue element, that tongue power, that world, that door, that ocean, that white, that field, that base, that near shore, that empty village — that is the tongue sense base. ^1-611

612. What is the body sense base? The sensitivity derived from the four great elements, included in the person, cannot be seen, can be pushed against, by which the body — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — touches touch that cannot be seen and can be pushed against, or has touched, touches, will touch, or might touch — that body, that body sense base, that body element, that body power, that world, that door, that ocean, that white, that field, that base, that near shore, that empty village — that is the body sense base. ^1-612

613. What is the body sense base? … in which the body … touch is pushed against … that is the body sense base. ^1-613

614. What is the body sense base? … by which the body … is pushed against in touch … that is the body sense base. ^1-614

615. What is the body sense base? … depending on which body, based on touch, body-contact arose … depending on which body, with touch as object, body-contact arose … feeling … perception … intention … body-consciousness arose — that body, that body sense base, that body element, that body power, that world, that door, that ocean, that white, that field, that base, that near shore, that empty village — that is the body sense base. ^1-615

616. What is the visible-form sense base? Visible form derived from the four great elements, colored, can be seen, can be pushed against — blue, yellow, red, white, black, crimson, green, parrot-colored, mango-sprout-colored, long, short, fine, coarse, round, circular, square, six-sided, eight-sided, sixteen-sided, hollow, raised, shadow, heat, light, darkness, mist, smoke, dust, the color of the moon's orb, the color of the sun's orb, the color of stars, the color of a mirror's orb, the color of a gem, coral, crystal, the color of born gold and silver, and whatever other visible form there may be derived from the four great elements, colored, can be seen, can be pushed against, which visible form — can be seen, can be pushed against — the eye — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — sees, has seen, will see, or might see — that visible form, that visible-form sense base, that visible-form element — that is the visible-form sense base. ^1-616

617. What is the visible-form sense base? … in which visible form … the eye … is pushed against … that is the visible-form sense base. ^1-617

618. What is the visible-form sense base? … by which the eye … is pushed against in visible form … that is the visible-form sense base. ^1-618

619. What is the visible-form sense base? … based on which visible form, depending on the eye, eye-contact arose … feeling … perception … intention … eye-consciousness arose … with visible form as object … that is the visible-form sense base. ^1-619

620. What is the sound sense base? Sound derived from the four great elements, cannot be seen, can be pushed against — drum sound, clay-drum sound, conch sound, horn sound, song sound, instrument sound, hand-clap sound, beings' uproar sound, elements' collision sound, wind sound, water sound, human sound, non-human sound, and whatever other sound there may be derived from the four great elements, cannot be seen, can be pushed against, which sound — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — the ear … hears, has heard, will hear, or might hear — that sound, that sound sense base, that sound element — that is the sound sense base. ^1-620

621. What is the sound sense base? … in which sound … the ear … is pushed against … that is the sound sense base. ^1-621

622. What is the sound sense base? … by which sound … is pushed against in the ear … that is the sound sense base. ^1-622

623. What is the sound sense base? … based on which sound, depending on the ear, ear-contact arose … with sound as object … feeling … perception … intention … ear-consciousness arose — that sound, that sound sense base, that sound element — that is the sound sense base. ^1-623

624. What is the smell sense base? Smell derived from the four great elements, cannot be seen, can be pushed against — root smell, heartwood smell, bark smell, leaf smell, flower smell, fruit smell, raw smell, cooked smell, good smell, bad smell, and whatever other smell there may be derived from the four great elements, cannot be seen, can be pushed against, which smell … the nose … smells … that smell, that smell sense base, that smell element — that is the smell sense base. ^1-624

625. What is the smell sense base? … in which smell … the nose … is pushed against … that is the smell sense base. ^1-625

626. What is the smell sense base? … by which smell … is pushed against in the nose … that is the smell sense base. ^1-626

627. What is the smell sense base? … based on which smell, depending on the nose, nose-contact arose … with smell as object … feeling … perception … intention … nose-consciousness arose — that smell, that smell sense base, that smell element — that is the smell sense base. ^1-627

628. What is the taste sense base? Taste derived from the four great elements, cannot be seen, can be pushed against — root taste, trunk taste, bark taste, leaf taste, flower taste, fruit taste, sour, sweet, bitter, pungent, salty, alkaline, astringent, pleasant, unpleasant, and whatever other taste there may be derived from the four great elements, cannot be seen, can be pushed against, which taste … the tongue … tastes … that taste, that taste sense base, that taste element — that is the taste sense base. ^1-628

629. What is the taste sense base? … in which taste … the tongue … is pushed against … that is the taste sense base. ^1-629

630. What is the taste sense base? … by which taste … is pushed against in the tongue … that is the taste sense base. ^1-630

631. What is the taste sense base? … based on which taste, depending on the tongue, tongue-contact arose … with taste as object … feeling … perception … intention … tongue-consciousness arose — that taste, that taste sense base, that taste element — that is the taste sense base. ^1-631

632. What is the female power? A woman's female mark, female sign, female formation, female manner, femaleness, being female — that is the female power. ^1-632

633. What is the male power? A man's male mark, male sign, male formation, male manner, maleness, being male — that is the male power. ^1-633

634. What is the life power? The life that sustains those form things — staying, maintaining, going on, keeping alive, protecting, life, life power — that is the life power. ^1-634

635. What is bodily communication? The stiffening, holding still, and state of being held still of the body when a wholesome mind, unwholesome mind, or indeterminate mind moves forward, moves back, looks ahead, looks aside, bends, stretches — communication, making known, state of being made known — that is bodily communication. ^1-635

636. What is verbal communication? Speech, voice, utterance, vocalization, sound, vocal act, speech, verbal expression — this is called speech. The communication, making known, and state of being made known through that speech — that is verbal communication. ^1-636

637. What is the space element? Space, what belongs to space, openness, what belongs to openness, a gap, what belongs to a gap, untouched by the four great elements — that is the space element. ^1-637

638. What is lightness of form? The lightness, easy change, not being heavy, not being thick of form — that is lightness of form. ^1-638

639. What is softness of form? The softness, tenderness, not being hard, not being rigid of form — that is softness of form. ^1-639

640. What is readiness of form? The readiness, workable state, and workability of form — that is readiness of form. ^1-640

641. What is growth of form? The heaping up of the sense bases — that is growth of form. ^1-641

642. What is continuity of form? The growth of form — that is continuity of form. ^1-642

643. What is aging of form? The aging, wearing out, breaking up, crumbling, wrinkled skin, the shrinking of life, the ripening of the powers of form — that is aging of form. ^1-643

644. What is impermanence of form? The breaking, falling, breaking apart, complete breaking, impermanence, disappearance of form — that is impermanence of form. ^1-644

645. What is physical food? Rice, porridge, barley meal, fish, meat, milk, curd, butter, fresh butter, oil, honey, molasses, and whatever other form there may be in whatever country as mouth-food, tooth-breaker, what is swallowed down the throat, what fills the stomach, the nutrient by which beings live — that is physical food.
This is form that is taken up.
Analysis by Taken-Up Form.
First Recitation Round in the Section on Form. ^1-645

646. What form is not taken up? Touch sense base, water element. ^1-646

647. What is the touch sense base? Earth element, fire element, air element — hard, soft, smooth, rough, pleasant touch, painful touch, heavy, light — whatever touch cannot be seen, can be pushed against, which touch — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — the body — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — touches, has touched, will touch, or might touch — that touch, that touch sense base, that touch element — that is the touch sense base. ^1-647

648. What is the touch sense base? Earth element, fire element, air element — hard, soft, smooth, rough, pleasant touch, painful touch, heavy, light — in which touch — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — the body — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — is pushed against, is being pushed against, will be pushed against, or might be pushed against — that touch, that touch sense base, that touch element — that is the touch sense base. ^1-648

649. What is the touch sense base? Earth element, fire element, air element — hard, soft, smooth, rough, pleasant touch, painful touch, heavy, light — which touch — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — in the body — cannot be seen, can be pushed against — is pushed against, is being pushed against, will be pushed against, or might be pushed against — that touch, that touch sense base, that touch element — that is the touch sense base. ^1-649

650. What is the touch sense base? Earth element, fire element, air element — hard, soft, smooth, rough, pleasant touch, painful touch, heavy, light — depending on which touch, based on the body, body-contact arose, is arising, will arise, or might arise … depending on which touch, based on the body, feeling … perception … intention … body-consciousness arose … depending on which touch, with the body as basis, body-contact arose … feeling … perception … intention … body-consciousness arose … depending on which touch, with touch as object, with the body as basis, body-contact arose … feeling … perception … intention … body-consciousness arose — that touch, that touch sense base, that touch element — that is the touch sense base. ^1-650

651. What is the water element? Water, what belongs to water, moisture, what belongs to moisture, the binding quality of form — that is the water element.
This is form that is not taken up. ^1-651

652. What form is taken up and can be clung to? Eye sense base, ear sense base, nose sense base, tongue sense base, body sense base, female power, male power, life power, and whatever other form there may be made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base, space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-652

653. What form is not taken up and cannot be clung to? Sound sense base, bodily communication, verbal communication, lightness of form, softness of form, readiness of form, aging of form, impermanence of form, and whatever other form there may be not made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base, space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is form not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-653

654. What form is taken up and can be clung to? Eye sense base … body sense base, female power, male power, life power, and whatever other form there may be made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base, space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-654

695. What is not the eye sense base? Ear sense base … physical food — that is not the eye sense base. ^1-695

717. What is not the male power? Eye sense base … physical food — that is not the male power. ^1-717

718. What is the life power? The life that sustains those form things — staying, maintaining, going on, keeping alive, protecting, life, life power — that is the life power. ^1-718

719. What is not the life power? Eye sense base … physical food — that is not the life power. ^1-719

The stiffening, holding still, and state of being held still of the body when a wholesome mind, unwholesome mind, or indeterminate mind moves forward, moves back, looks ahead, looks aside, bends, stretches — communication, making known, state of being made known — that is bodily communication. ^1-720

742. What is physical food? Rice, porridge, barley meal, fish, meat, milk, curd, butter, fresh butter, oil, honey, molasses, and whatever other form there may be in whatever country as mouth-food, tooth-breaker, what is swallowed down the throat, what fills the stomach, the nutrient by which beings live — that is physical food. ^1-742

Thus the collection of form in two ways.
Dual Analysis. ^1-743

##### Triple Analysis ^1-2-3-4-0

744. What form is internal and taken up? Eye sense base … body sense base — that is internal form that is taken up. ^1-744

745. What form is external and taken up? Visible-form sense base … physical food — that is external form that is taken up. ^1-745

746. What form is external and not taken up? Touch sense base, water element — that is external form that is not taken up. ^1-746

747. What form is internal and taken up and can be clung to? Eye sense base … body sense base — that is internal form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-747

748. What form is external and taken up and can be clung to? Female power, male power, life power, and whatever other form there may be made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base, space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is external form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-748

749. What form is external and not taken up and cannot be clung to? Sound sense base, bodily communication, verbal communication, lightness of form, softness of form, readiness of form, aging of form, impermanence of form, and whatever other form there may be not made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base, space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is external form not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-749

755. What form is external and cannot be seen? Sound sense base … physical food — that is external form that cannot be seen. ^1-755

760. What form is external and is a power? Female power, male power, life power — that is external form that is a power. ^1-760

761. What form is external and is not a power? Visible-form sense base … physical food — that is external form that is not a power. ^1-761

767. What form is external and is not communication? Visible-form sense base … physical food — that is external form that is not communication. ^1-767

Bodily communication, verbal communication, and whatever other form there may be born of mind, with mind as cause, arising from mind — visible-form sense base, sound sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base, space element, water element, lightness of form, softness of form, readiness of form, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is external form that arises from mind. ^1-769

880. What form is external and is physical food? Rice, porridge, barley meal, fish, meat, milk, curd, butter, fresh butter, oil, honey, molasses, and whatever other form there may be in whatever country as mouth-food, tooth-breaker, what is swallowed down the throat, what fills the stomach, the nutrient by which beings live — that is external physical food. ^1-880

881. What form is external and is not physical food? Visible-form sense base … impermanence of form — that is external form that is not physical food.
Thus the collection of form in three ways.
Triple Analysis. ^1-881

932. What form is not taken up and cannot be clung to and is a great element? Touch sense base not made by action, water element — that is form not taken up and cannot be clung to that is a great element. ^1-932

933. What form is not taken up and cannot be clung to and is not a great element? Sound sense base, bodily communication, verbal communication, lightness of form, softness of form, readiness of form, aging of form, impermanence of form, and whatever other form there may be not made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, space element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is form not taken up and cannot be clung to that is not a great element. ^1-933

934. What form is taken up and can be clung to and coarse? Eye sense base … body sense base, and whatever other form there may be made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base — that is coarse form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-934

935. What form is taken up and can be clung to and fine? Female power, male power, life power, and whatever other form there may be made by action — space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is fine form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-935

936. What form is not taken up and cannot be clung to and coarse? Sound sense base, and whatever other form there may be not made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base — that is coarse form not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-936

937. What form is not taken up and cannot be clung to and fine? Bodily communication, verbal communication, lightness of form, softness of form, readiness of form, aging of form, impermanence of form, and whatever other form there may be not made by action — space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is fine form not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-937

938. What form is taken up and can be clung to and far? Female power, male power, life power, and whatever other form there may be made by action — space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is far form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-938

939. What form is taken up and can be clung to and near? Eye sense base … body sense base, and whatever other form there may be made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base — that is near form taken up and can be clung to. ^1-939

940. What form is not taken up and cannot be clung to and far? Bodily communication, verbal communication, lightness of form, softness of form, readiness of form, aging of form, impermanence of form, and whatever other form there may be not made by action — space element, water element, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — that is far form not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-940

941. What form is not taken up and cannot be clung to and near? Sound sense base, and whatever other form there may be not made by action — visible-form sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base — that is near form not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-941

942. What form that can be pushed against is a power? Eye power … body power — that is form that can be pushed against and is a power. ^1-942

943. What form that can be pushed against is not a power? Visible-form sense base … touch sense base — that is form that can be pushed against and is not a power. ^1-943

944. What form that cannot be pushed against is a power? Female power, male power, life power — that is form that cannot be pushed against and is a power. ^1-944

945. What form that cannot be pushed against is not a power? Bodily communication … physical food — that is form that cannot be pushed against and is not a power. ^1-945

946. What form that can be pushed against is a great element? Touch sense base — that is form that can be pushed against and is a great element. ^1-946

947. What form that can be pushed against is not a great element? Eye sense base … taste sense base — that is form that can be pushed against and is not a great element. ^1-947

948. What form that cannot be pushed against is a great element? Water element — that is form that cannot be pushed against and is a great element. ^1-948

949. What form that cannot be pushed against is not a great element? Female power … physical food — that is form that cannot be pushed against and is not a great element. ^1-949

950. What form that is a power is coarse? Eye power … body power — that is coarse form that is a power. ^1-950

951. What form that is a power is fine? Female power, male power, life power — that is fine form that is a power. ^1-951

952. What form that is not a power is coarse? Visible-form sense base … touch sense base — that is coarse form that is not a power. ^1-952

953. What form that is not a power is fine? Bodily communication … physical food — that is fine form that is not a power. ^1-953

954. What form that is a power is far? Female power, male power, life power — that is far form that is a power. ^1-954

955. What form that is a power is near? Eye power … body power — that is near form that is a power. ^1-955

956. What form that is not a power is far? Bodily communication … physical food — that is far form that is not a power. ^1-956

957. What form that is not a power is near? Visible-form sense base … touch sense base — that is near form that is not a power. ^1-957

958. What form that is a great element is coarse? Touch sense base — that is coarse form that is a great element. ^1-958

959. What form that is a great element is fine? Water element — that is fine form that is a great element. ^1-959

960. What form that is not a great element is coarse? Eye sense base … taste sense base — that is coarse form that is not a great element. ^1-960

961. What form that is not a great element is fine? Female power … physical food — that is fine form that is not a great element. ^1-961

962. What form that is a great element is far? Water element — that is far form that is a great element. ^1-962

963. What form that is a great element is near? Touch sense base — that is near form that is a great element. ^1-963

964. What form that is not a great element is far? Female power … physical food — that is far form that is not a great element. ^1-964

965. What form that is not a great element is near? Eye sense base … taste sense base — that is near form that is not a great element. ^1-965

966. Visible-form sense base is seen; sound sense base is heard; smell sense base, taste sense base, and touch sense base are sensed; all form is known by mind.
Thus the collection of form in four ways.
Quadruplets. ^1-966

##### Fives ^1-2-3-6-0

967. What is the earth element? Whatever is hard, roughness, hardness, the state of being hard — internal or external, taken up and can be clung to or not taken up and cannot be clung to — that is the earth element. ^1-967

968. What is the water element? Water, what belongs to water, moisture, what belongs to moisture, the binding quality of form — internal or external, taken up and can be clung to or not taken up and cannot be clung to — that is the water element. ^1-968

969. What is the fire element? Fire, what belongs to fire, heat, what belongs to heat, warmth, what belongs to warmth — internal or external, taken up and can be clung to or not taken up and cannot be clung to — that is the fire element. ^1-969

970. What is the air element? Air, what belongs to air, the stiffening quality of form — internal or external, taken up and can be clung to or not taken up and cannot be clung to — that is the air element. ^1-970

971. What form is taken up? Eye sense base … physical food — that is form that is taken up.
Thus the collection of form in five ways.
Fives. ^1-971

##### Sixes ^1-2-3-7-0

972. Visible-form sense base is form knowable by eye-consciousness; sound sense base is form knowable by ear-consciousness; smell sense base is form knowable by nose-consciousness; taste sense base is form knowable by tongue-consciousness; touch sense base is form knowable by body-consciousness; all form is form knowable by mind-consciousness.
Thus the collection of form in six ways.
Sixes. ^1-972

##### Sevens ^1-2-3-8-0

973. Visible-form sense base is form knowable by eye-consciousness; sound sense base is form knowable by ear-consciousness; smell sense base is form knowable by nose-consciousness; taste sense base is form knowable by tongue-consciousness; touch sense base is form knowable by body-consciousness; visible-form sense base, sound sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, and touch sense base are form knowable by the mind element; all form is form knowable by the mind-consciousness element.
Thus the collection of form in seven ways.
Sevens. ^1-973

##### Eights ^1-2-3-9-0

974. Visible-form sense base is form knowable by eye-consciousness; sound sense base is form knowable by ear-consciousness; smell sense base is form knowable by nose-consciousness; taste sense base is form knowable by tongue-consciousness; pleasant touch, pleasant contact, is form knowable by body-consciousness; unpleasant touch, painful contact, is form knowable by body-consciousness; visible-form sense base, sound sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, and touch sense base are form knowable by the mind element; all form is form knowable by the mind-consciousness element.
Thus the collection of form in eight ways.
Eights. ^1-974

##### Nines ^1-2-3-10-0

975. What is the eye power? The sensitivity derived from the four great elements … empty village — that is the eye power. ^1-975

976. What is the ear power … nose power … tongue power … body power … female power … male power … life power? The life that sustains those form things — staying, maintaining, going on, keeping alive, protecting, life, life power — that is the life power. ^1-976

977. What form is not a power? Visible-form sense base … physical food — that is form that is not a power.
Thus the collection of form in nine ways.
Nines. ^1-977

##### Tens ^1-2-3-11-0

978. What is the eye power? The sensitivity derived from the four great elements … empty village — that is the eye power. ^1-978

979. What is the ear power … nose power … tongue power … body power … female power … male power … life power? The life that sustains those form things — staying, maintaining, going on, keeping alive, protecting, life, life power — that is the life power. ^1-979

980. What form that is not a power can be pushed against? Visible-form sense base … touch sense base — that is form that is not a power and can be pushed against. ^1-980

981. What form that is not a power cannot be pushed against? Bodily communication … physical food — that is form that is not a power and cannot be pushed against.
Thus the collection of form in ten ways.
Tens. ^1-981

##### Elevens ^1-2-3-12-0

982. What is the eye sense base? The sensitivity derived from the four great elements … empty village — that is the eye sense base. ^1-982

983. What is the ear sense base … nose sense base … tongue sense base … body sense base … visible-form sense base … sound sense base … smell sense base … taste sense base … touch sense base? Earth element … touch element — that is the touch sense base. ^1-983

984. What form cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against and is included in the mental-object sense base? Female power … physical food — that is form that cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against and is included in the mental-object sense base.
Thus the collection of form in eleven ways.
Elevens.
Eighth Recitation Round.
Analysis of Form.
The Section on Form is finished. ^1-984

### Summary Section ^1-3-0

#### Triple Summary ^1-3-1-0

985. What things are good for the mind? The three roots of what is good for the mind — non-greed, non-hatred, wisdom without delusion; the aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are good for the mind. ^1-985

986. What things are bad for the mind? The three roots of what is bad for the mind — greed, hatred, delusion; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are bad for the mind. ^1-986

987. What things are neither good nor bad for the mind? Results of things good and bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness; things that are functional — neither good nor bad for the mind nor results of action; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are neither good nor bad for the mind. ^1-987

988. What things are united with pleasant feeling? In the pleasant level of the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except pleasant feeling itself; the aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are united with pleasant feeling. ^1-988

989. What things are united with painful feeling? In the painful level of the sense-desire level, except painful feeling itself; the aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are united with painful feeling. ^1-989

990. What things are united with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling? In the neither-painful-nor-pleasant level of the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included, except neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling itself; the aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are united with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling. ^1-990

991. What things are results? Results of things good and bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are results. ^1-991

992. What things are things that produce result? Things good and bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are things that produce result. ^1-992

993. What things are neither results nor things that produce result? Things that are functional — neither good nor bad for the mind nor results of action; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are neither results nor things that produce result. ^1-993

994. What things are taken up and can be clung to? Results of things good and bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; and whatever form is made by action — these things are taken up and can be clung to. ^1-994

995. What things are not taken up and cannot be clung to? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; things that are functional — neither good nor bad for the mind nor results of action; and whatever form is not made by action — these things are not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-995

996. What things are not taken up and cannot be clung to? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-996

997. What things are stained and stain? The three roots of what is bad for the mind — greed, hatred, delusion; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are stained and stain. ^1-997

998. What things are unstained and stain? Things good and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; the aggregate of form, aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness — these things are unstained and stain. ^1-998

999. What things are unstained and do not stain? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are unstained and do not stain. ^1-999

1000. What things have thinking and examining? In the level with thinking and examining, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except thinking and examining themselves; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things have thinking and examining. ^1-1000

1001. What things have only examining? In the level with only examining, belonging to the form level and what is not included, except examining itself; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things have only examining. ^1-1001

1002. What things have no thinking and no examining? In the level without thinking and without examining, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things have no thinking and no examining. ^1-1002

1003. What things are accompanied by a feeling of joy? In the level with a feeling of joy, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except the feeling of joy itself; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are accompanied by a feeling of joy. ^1-1003

1004. What things are accompanied by happiness? In the pleasant level, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except happiness itself; the aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are accompanied by happiness. ^1-1004

1005. What things are accompanied by calm balance? In the level with calm balance, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included, except calm balance itself; the aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are accompanied by calm balance. ^1-1005

1006. What things are to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters — personality view, doubt, misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1006

1007. What is personality view? Here an untaught ordinary person who has not seen the noble ones, is unskilled and untrained in the noble teaching, has not seen good people, is unskilled and untrained in the good-person teaching, regards form as the self, or the self as having form, or form as in the self, or the self as in form; regards feeling as the self … perception as the self … habits and intentions as the self … consciousness as the self, or the self as having consciousness, or consciousness as in the self, or the self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind — view, being of the nature of view, jungle of views, thicket of views, distortion of views, writhing of views, fetter of views, grasping, taking up, settling in, clinging, wrong path, wrongness, wrong place, upside-down grasping — this is called personality view. ^1-1007

1008. What is doubt? Doubt and wavering about the teacher, about the teaching, about the community, about the training, about the past, about the future, about past and future together, and about things that arise from supporting conditions in dependence on causes. Whatever doubt and wavering of this kind — uncertainty, being in two minds, double path, indecision, not taking one side, shrinking back, shrinking away, not going forward, stiffness, confusion of mind — this is called doubt. ^1-1008

1009. What is misbelief in rites and vows? The view of recluses and brahmins outside this teaching that “purity comes from virtue, purity comes from practice, purity comes from virtue and practice.” Whatever view of this kind — view, being of the nature of view, jungle of views … upside-down grasping — this is called misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1009

1010. These three fetters; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1010

1011. What things are to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hatred, and delusion; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are to be abandoned by development. ^1-1011

1012. What things are not to be abandoned by seeing nor by development? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not to be abandoned by seeing nor by development. ^1-1012

1013. What things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters — personality view, doubt, misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1013

1014. What is personality view? … this is called personality view. ^1-1014

1015. What is doubt? … this is called doubt. ^1-1015

1016. What is misbelief in rites and vows? … this is called misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1016

1017. These three fetters; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. The three fetters — personality view, doubt, misbelief in rites and vows — these things are to be abandoned by seeing. Greed, hatred, and delusion of the same class — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. Defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1017

1018. What things are causes of things to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hatred, and delusion — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by development. Defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by development. ^1-1018

1019. What things are not causes of things to be abandoned by seeing nor by development? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not causes of things to be abandoned by seeing nor by development. ^1-1019

1020. What things lead to increase? Things good and bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things lead to increase. ^1-1020

1021. What things lead to decrease? The four paths that are not included — these things lead to decrease. ^1-1021

1022. What things neither lead to increase nor to decrease? Results of things good and bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; things that are functional — neither good nor bad for the mind nor results of action; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things neither lead to increase nor to decrease. ^1-1022

1023. What things belong to one in training? The four paths that are not included, and the three lower noble-fruition results — these things belong to one in training. ^1-1023

1024. What things belong to one beyond training? The highest noble-fruition result of full awakening — these things belong to one beyond training. ^1-1024

1025. What things neither belong to one in training nor beyond training? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things neither belong to one in training nor beyond training. ^1-1025

1026. What things are small? All things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level; the aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are small. ^1-1026

1027. What things are great? Things good and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the form level and formless level; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are great. ^1-1027

1028. What things are unlimited? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are unlimited. ^1-1028

1029. What things have small objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on small things — these things have small objects. ^1-1029

1030. What things have great objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on great things — these things have great objects. ^1-1030

1031. What things have unlimited objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on unlimited things — these things have unlimited objects. ^1-1031

1032. What things are inferior? The three roots of what is bad for the mind — greed, hatred, delusion; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are inferior. ^1-1032

1033. What things are middling? Things good and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; the aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are middling. ^1-1033

1034. What things are excellent? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are excellent. ^1-1034

1035. What things have fixed wrongness? The five actions with immediate result, and fixed wrong view — these things have fixed wrongness. ^1-1035

1036. What things have fixed rightness? The four paths that are not included — these things have fixed rightness. ^1-1036

1037. What things are not fixed? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not fixed. ^1-1037

1038. What things have the path as object? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on the noble path — these things have the path as object. ^1-1038

1039. What things have the path as cause? For one who possesses the noble path, except the path factors; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things have the path as cause. For one who possesses the noble path, right view is both path and cause; except right view, the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany it — these things have the path as cause. For one who possesses the noble path, non-greed, non-hatred, and wisdom without delusion — these things are causes of the path. The aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things have the path as cause. ^1-1039

1040. What things are dominated by the path? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise making the noble path dominant — these things are dominated by the path. For one who possesses the noble path and develops the path dominated by investigation, except investigation; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany it — these things are dominated by the path. ^1-1040

1041. What things have arisen? Things that have been born, become, arisen, appeared, come to be, come forth, stood up, sprung up, arisen, included under what has arisen — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things have arisen. ^1-1041

1042. What things have not arisen? Things that have not been born, not become, not arisen, not appeared, not come to be, not come forth, not stood up, not sprung up, not arisen, included under what has not arisen — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things have not arisen. ^1-1042

1043. What things are producers? Results of things good and bad for the mind that are not matured results, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; and whatever form made by action will arise — these things are producers. ^1-1043

1044. What things are past? Things that are past, ceased, gone, changed, fallen, disappeared, having arisen and gone, included under the past — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things are past. ^1-1044

1045. What things are future? Things that have not been born … included under the future — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things are future. ^1-1045

1046. What things are present? Things that have been born … included under the present — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things are present. ^1-1046

1047. What things have past objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on past things — these things have past objects. ^1-1047

1048. What things have future objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on future things — these things have future objects. ^1-1048

1049. What things have present objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on present things — these things have present objects. ^1-1049

1050. What things are internal? Things that are internal, personal, fixed, individual, taken up, of those beings — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things are internal. ^1-1050

1051. What things are external? Things that are internal, personal, fixed, individual, taken up, of other beings, of other persons — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things are external. ^1-1051

1052. What things are internal and external? Both together — these things are internal and external. ^1-1052

1053. What things have internal objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on internal things — these things have internal objects. ^1-1053

1054. What things have external objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on external things — these things have external objects. ^1-1054

1055. What things have internal and external objects? Mind-and-mental-factor things that arise based on internal and external things — these things have internal and external objects. ^1-1055

1056. What things can be seen and can be pushed against? Visible-form sense base — these things can be seen and can be pushed against. ^1-1056

1057. What things cannot be seen and can be pushed against? Eye sense base, ear sense base, nose sense base, tongue sense base, body sense base, sound sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base — these things cannot be seen and can be pushed against. ^1-1057

1058. What things cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against? The aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness; whatever form cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against that is included in the mental-object sense base; and the unconditioned element — these things cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against.
Triples. ^1-1058

#### Dual Summary ^1-3-2-0

##### Causes Group ^1-3-2-1-0

1059. What things are causes? Three wholesome causes, three unwholesome causes, three indeterminate causes, nine sense-desire-level causes, six form-level causes, six formless-level causes, six causes that are not included. ^1-1059

1060. What are the three wholesome causes? Non-greed, non-hatred, wisdom without delusion. ^1-1060

1061. What is non-greed? Not being greedy, state of not being greedy, without lust, without being lustful, state of not being lustful, without coveting — non-greed, a root of what is good for the mind — this is called non-greed. ^1-1061

1062. What is non-hatred? Not hating, state of not hating, friendliness, being friendly, state of being friendly, without malice, without acting with malice, state of not acting with malice, wishing well, compassion, without ill will, without harming — non-hatred, a root of what is good for the mind — this is called non-hatred. ^1-1062

1063. What is wisdom without delusion? Knowledge of suffering, knowledge of the origin of suffering, knowledge of the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the path leading to the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the past, knowledge of the future, knowledge of past and future together, knowledge of things that arise from supporting conditions in dependence on causes — whatever wisdom of this kind: discernment, examining, investigation, analysis of things, marking, noting, full noting, skill, proficiency, expertness, reflection, inquiry, breadth, intelligence, guidance, insight, clear comprehension, spur, wisdom, power of wisdom, strength of wisdom, sword of wisdom, fortress of wisdom, light of wisdom, radiance of wisdom, jewel of wisdom — wisdom without delusion, analysis of things, right view — this is called wisdom without delusion.
These are the three wholesome causes. ^1-1063

1064. What are the three unwholesome causes? Greed, hatred, delusion. ^1-1064

1065. What is greed? Lust, being lustful, following along, going along with, delight, delight in lust, thirst for sense pleasures, fondness for sense pleasures, flooding with sense pleasures, intoxication with sense pleasures, clinging to sense pleasures, thirst for becoming, thirst for non-becoming, thirst for form, thirst for the formless, thirst for cessation, thirst for visible form, thirst for sound, thirst for smell, thirst for taste, thirst for touch, thirst for mental objects, flood, yoke, bond, clinging, obstruction, hindrance, covering, binding, stain, underlying tendency, outbreak, creeper, dissatisfaction, root of suffering, source of suffering, origin of suffering, snare of the destroyer, bait of the destroyer, domain of the destroyer, river of thirst, net of thirst, cord of thirst, ocean of thirst, coveting — greed, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called greed. ^1-1065

1066. What is hatred? “He did me wrong” — anger arises; “he is doing me wrong” — anger arises; “he will do me wrong” — anger arises; “he did wrong to what is dear and pleasing to me” … “he is doing wrong” … “he will do wrong” — anger arises; “he did good to what is not dear and not pleasing to me” … “he is doing good” … “he will do good” — anger arises; or anger arises where it should not. Whatever anger of this kind in the mind — ill will, repulsion, opposing, rage, great rage, extreme rage, hatred, malice, great malice, harm in the mind, ill will in the mind, anger, being angry, state of being angry, hatred, hating, state of hating, harm, harming, state of harming, opposition, opposing, fierceness, being like a demon, discontent of mind — this is called hatred. ^1-1066

1067. What is delusion? Not knowing suffering, not knowing the origin of suffering, not knowing the cessation of suffering, not knowing the path leading to the cessation of suffering, not knowing the past, not knowing the future, not knowing past and future together, not knowing things that arise from supporting conditions in dependence on causes — whatever not-knowing of this kind: not seeing, not understanding, not penetrating, not discerning, not taking up, not going over, not considering, not reviewing, not doing the work of knowing, dullness, foolishness, without clear comprehension, delusion, great delusion, complete delusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, yoke of ignorance, underlying tendency of ignorance, outbreak of ignorance, banner of ignorance — delusion, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called delusion.
These are the three unwholesome causes. ^1-1067

1068. What are the three indeterminate causes? From results of things good for the mind, or from functional indeterminate things — non-greed, non-hatred, wisdom without delusion — these are the three indeterminate causes. ^1-1068

1069. What are the nine sense-desire-level causes? Three wholesome causes, three unwholesome causes, three indeterminate causes — these are the nine sense-desire-level causes. ^1-1069

1070. What are the six form-level causes? Three wholesome causes, three indeterminate causes — these are the six form-level causes. ^1-1070

1071. What are the six formless-level causes? Three wholesome causes, three indeterminate causes — these are the six formless-level causes. ^1-1071

1072. What are the six causes that are not included? Three wholesome causes, three indeterminate causes — these are the six causes that are not included. ^1-1072

1073. What are the three wholesome causes? Non-greed, non-hatred, wisdom without delusion. ^1-1073

1074. What is non-greed? Not being greedy, state of not being greedy, without lust, without being lustful, state of not being lustful, without coveting — non-greed, a root of what is good for the mind — this is called non-greed. ^1-1074

1075. What is non-hatred? Not hating, state of not hating … without harming — non-hatred, a root of what is good for the mind — this is called non-hatred. ^1-1075

1076. What is wisdom without delusion? Knowledge of suffering … jewel of wisdom — wisdom without delusion, analysis of things, right view, awakening factor of analysis of things, path factor, included in the path — this is called wisdom without delusion.
These are the three wholesome causes. ^1-1076

1077. What are the three indeterminate causes? From results of things good for the mind — non-greed, non-hatred, wisdom without delusion — these are the three indeterminate causes. These six causes that are not included — these things are causes. ^1-1077

1078. What things are not causes? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not causes. ^1-1078

1079. What things have causes? Things that have those things as causes — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things have causes. ^1-1079

1080. What things have no causes? Things that have no causes — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things have no causes. ^1-1080

1081. What things are united with causes? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with causes. ^1-1081

1082. What things are separated from causes? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from causes. ^1-1082

1083. What things are both causes and have causes? Greed is both cause and has cause with delusion; delusion is both cause and has cause with greed; hatred is both cause and has cause with delusion; delusion is both cause and has cause with hatred; non-greed, non-hatred, and wisdom without delusion are each other's causes and have causes — these things are both causes and have causes. ^1-1083

1084. What things have causes but are not causes? Things that have causes, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things have causes but are not causes. ^1-1084

1085. What things are both causes and united with causes? Greed is both cause and united with cause with delusion; delusion is both cause and united with cause with greed; hatred is both cause and united with cause with delusion; delusion is both cause and united with cause with hatred; non-greed, non-hatred, and wisdom without delusion are each other's causes and united with causes — these things are both causes and united with causes. ^1-1085

1086. What things are united with causes but are not causes? Things united with those things, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with causes but are not causes. ^1-1086

1087. What things are not causes but have causes? Things that are not causes but have causes — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are not causes but have causes. ^1-1087

1088. What things are not causes and have no causes? Things that are not causes and have no causes — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not causes and have no causes. ^1-1088

##### Short Interior Duals ^1-3-2-2-0

1089. What things have supporting conditions? The five aggregates — aggregate of form, aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, aggregate of consciousness — these things have supporting conditions. ^1-1089

1090. What things have no supporting conditions? The unconditioned element — these things have no supporting conditions. ^1-1090

1091. What things are made? Those very things that have supporting conditions — those very things are made. ^1-1091

1092. What things are unmade? That very thing that has no supporting conditions — that very thing is unmade. ^1-1092

1093. What things can be seen? Visible-form sense base — these things can be seen. ^1-1093

1094. What things cannot be seen? Eye sense base … touch sense base, aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness, whatever form cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against that is included in the mental-object sense base, and the unconditioned element — these things cannot be seen. ^1-1094

1095. What things can be pushed against? Eye sense base … touch sense base — these things can be pushed against. ^1-1095

1096. What things cannot be pushed against? Aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness, whatever form cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against that is included in the mental-object sense base, and the unconditioned element — these things cannot be pushed against. ^1-1096

1097. What things have form? The four great elements and form derived from the four great elements — these things have form. ^1-1097

1098. What things are formless? Aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness, and the unconditioned element — these things are formless. ^1-1098

1099. What things are worldly? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are worldly. ^1-1099

1100. What things are supramundane? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are supramundane. ^1-1100

1101. What things are knowable by some and not knowable by some? Those things knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness. Those knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness. Those knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness. Those knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness. Those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness. Those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness. Those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness. Those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; those knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness. Those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness. Those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness. Those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; those knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness. Those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness. Those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness; those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness. Those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; those knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness. Those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness. Those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness. Those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by eye-consciousness; those knowable by eye-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness. Those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by ear-consciousness; those knowable by ear-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness. Those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by nose-consciousness; those knowable by nose-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness. Those knowable by body-consciousness are not knowable by tongue-consciousness; those knowable by tongue-consciousness are not knowable by body-consciousness. These things are knowable by some and not knowable by some. ^1-1101

##### Floods Group ^1-3-2-3-0

1102. What things are floods? The four floods — sense-desire flood, becoming flood, view flood, ignorance flood. ^1-1102

1103. What is the sense-desire flood? Sense-desire in sense pleasures, lust for sense pleasures, delight in sense pleasures, thirst for sense pleasures, fondness for sense pleasures, flooding with sense pleasures, intoxication with sense pleasures, clinging to sense pleasures — this is called the sense-desire flood. ^1-1103

1104. What is the becoming flood? Desire for becoming in states of becoming, lust for becoming, delight in becoming, thirst for becoming, fondness for becoming, flooding with becoming, intoxication with becoming, clinging to becoming — this is called the becoming flood. ^1-1104

1105. What is the view flood? “The world is eternal,” or “the world is not eternal,” or “the world is finite,” or “the world is infinite,” or “the soul is the body,” or “the soul is one thing and the body another,” or “the Realized One exists after death,” or “the Realized One does not exist after death,” or “the Realized One both exists and does not exist after death,” or “the Realized One neither exists nor does not exist after death”; whatever view of this kind — view, being of the nature of view, jungle of views … upside-down grasping — this is called the view flood. All wrong view is the view flood. ^1-1105

1106. What is the ignorance flood? Not knowing suffering … banner of ignorance — delusion, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called the ignorance flood.
These things are floods. ^1-1106

1107. What things are not floods? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not floods. ^1-1107

1108. What things have mental stains? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things have mental stains. ^1-1108

1109. What things have no mental stains? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things have no mental stains. ^1-1109

1110. What things are united with floods? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with floods. ^1-1110

1111. What things are separated from floods? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from floods. ^1-1111

1112. What things are both floods and have mental stains? Those very floods are both floods and have mental stains. ^1-1112

1113. What things have mental stains but are not floods? Things that have mental stains, except those things — the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things have mental stains but are not floods. ^1-1113

1114. What things are both floods and united with floods? The sense-desire flood is both flood and united with flood with the ignorance flood; the ignorance flood is both flood and united with flood with the sense-desire flood; the becoming flood is both flood and united with flood with the ignorance flood; the ignorance flood is both flood and united with flood with the becoming flood; the view flood is both flood and united with flood with the ignorance flood; the ignorance flood is both flood and united with flood with the view flood — these things are both floods and united with floods. ^1-1114

1115. What things are united with floods but are not floods? Things united with those things, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with floods but are not floods. ^1-1115

1116. What things are separated from floods and have mental stains? Things separated from those things that have mental stains — things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are separated from floods and have mental stains. ^1-1116

1117. What things are separated from floods and have no mental stains? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from floods and have no mental stains.
First Recitation Round in the Summary Section. ^1-1117

##### Fetters Group ^1-3-2-4-0

1118. What things are fetters? The ten fetters — fetter of lust for sense pleasures, fetter of repulsion, fetter of pride, fetter of views, fetter of doubt, fetter of misbelief in rites and vows, fetter of lust for becoming, fetter of jealousy, fetter of stinginess, fetter of ignorance. ^1-1118

1119. What is the fetter of lust for sense pleasures? Sense-desire in sense pleasures, lust for sense pleasures, delight in sense pleasures, thirst for sense pleasures, fondness for sense pleasures, flooding with sense pleasures, intoxication with sense pleasures, clinging to sense pleasures — this is called the fetter of lust for sense pleasures. ^1-1119

1120. What is the fetter of repulsion? “He did me wrong” — anger arises … discontent of mind — this is called the fetter of repulsion. ^1-1120

1121. What is the fetter of pride? “I am superior” pride, “I am equal” pride, “I am inferior” pride — whatever pride of this kind: conceiving, state of conceiving, uplift, upliftedness, banner, pretension, desire for recognition in the mind — this is called the fetter of pride. ^1-1121

1122. What is the fetter of views? “The world is eternal,” or “the world is not eternal,” … “the Realized One neither exists nor does not exist after death”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called the fetter of views. Except the fetter of misbelief in rites and vows, all wrong view is the fetter of views. ^1-1122

1123. What is the fetter of doubt? Doubt and wavering about the teacher, about the teaching, about the community, about the training, about the past, about the future, about past and future together, and about things that arise from supporting conditions in dependence on causes … confusion of mind — this is called the fetter of doubt. ^1-1123

1124. What is the fetter of misbelief in rites and vows? The view of recluses and brahmins outside this teaching that “purity comes from virtue, purity comes from practice, purity comes from virtue and practice”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called the fetter of misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1124

1125. What is the fetter of lust for becoming? Desire for becoming in states of becoming, lust for becoming, delight in becoming, thirst for becoming, fondness for becoming, flooding with becoming, intoxication with becoming, clinging to becoming — this is called the fetter of lust for becoming. ^1-1125

1126. What is the fetter of jealousy? Envy, being envious, state of being envious, grudging, being grudging, state of being grudging at others' gain, honor, respect, veneration, and worship — this is called the fetter of jealousy. ^1-1126

1127. What is the fetter of stinginess? The five stinginesses — stinginess about dwelling, stinginess about family, stinginess about gain, stinginess about appearance, stinginess about teaching. Whatever stinginess of this kind: being stingy, state of being stingy, dissatisfaction, miserliness, tight-heartedness, graspingness of mind — this is called the fetter of stinginess. ^1-1127

1128. What is the fetter of ignorance? Not knowing suffering … delusion, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called the fetter of ignorance.
These things are fetters. ^1-1128

1129. What things are not fetters? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not fetters. ^1-1129

1130. What things are fetterable? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are fetterable. ^1-1130

1131. What things are not fetterable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not fetterable. ^1-1131

1132. What things are united with fetters? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with fetters. ^1-1132

1133. What things are separated from fetters? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from fetters. ^1-1133

1134. What things are both fetters and fetterable? Those very fetters are both fetters and fetterable. ^1-1134

1135. What things are fetterable but are not fetters? Things that are fetterable, except those things — the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are fetterable but are not fetters. ^1-1135

1136. What things are both fetters and united with fetters? The fetter of lust for sense pleasures is both fetter and united with fetter with the fetter of ignorance … the fetter of stinginess is both fetter and united with fetter with the fetter of ignorance, and the fetter of ignorance is both fetter and united with fetter with the fetter of stinginess — these things are both fetters and united with fetters. ^1-1136

1137. What things are united with fetters but are not fetters? Things united with those things, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with fetters but are not fetters. ^1-1137

1138. What things are separated from fetters and fetterable? Things separated from those things that are fetterable — things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are separated from fetters and fetterable. ^1-1138

1139. What things are separated from fetters and not fetterable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from fetters and not fetterable. ^1-1139

##### Bonds Group ^1-3-2-5-0

1140. What things are bonds? The four bonds — bond of coveting, bond of ill will, bond of misbelief in rites and vows, bond of clinging to this as the truth. ^1-1140

1141. What is the bond of coveting? Lust, being lustful … coveting, greed, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called the bond of coveting. ^1-1141

1142. What is the bond of ill will? “He did me wrong” — anger arises … discontent of mind — this is called the bond of ill will. ^1-1142

1143. What is the bond of misbelief in rites and vows? The view of recluses and brahmins outside this teaching that “purity comes from virtue, purity comes from practice, purity comes from virtue and practice”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called the bond of misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1143

1144. What is the bond of clinging to this as the truth? “The world is eternal — only this is true, the rest is worthless,” or “the world is not eternal — only this is true, the rest is worthless,” or “the world is finite … infinite … the soul is the body … the soul is one thing and the body another … the Realized One exists after death … does not exist … both exists and does not exist … neither exists nor does not exist — only this is true, the rest is worthless”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called the bond of clinging to this as the truth. Except the bond of misbelief in rites and vows, all wrong view is the bond of clinging to this as the truth.
These things are bonds. ^1-1144

1145. What things are not bonds? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not bonds. ^1-1145

1146. What things are bondable? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are bondable. ^1-1146

1147. What things are not bondable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not bondable. ^1-1147

1148. What things are united with bonds? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with bonds. ^1-1148

1149. What things are separated from bonds? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from bonds. ^1-1149

1150. What things are both bonds and bondable? Those very bonds are both bonds and bondable. ^1-1150

1151. What things are bondable but are not bonds? Things that are bondable, except those things — the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are bondable but are not bonds. ^1-1151

1152. What things are both bonds and united with bonds? The bond of misbelief in rites and vows is both bond and united with bond with the bond of coveting; the bond of coveting is both bond and united with bond with the bond of misbelief in rites and vows; the bond of clinging to this as the truth is both bond and united with bond with the bond of coveting; the bond of coveting is both bond and united with bond with the bond of clinging to this as the truth — these things are both bonds and united with bonds. ^1-1152

1153. What things are united with bonds but are not bonds? Things united with those things, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with bonds but are not bonds. ^1-1153

1154. What things are separated from bonds and bondable? Things separated from those things that are bondable — things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are separated from bonds and bondable. ^1-1154
