1155. What things are separated from bonds and not bondable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from bonds and not bondable. ^1-1155

##### Floods Group (summary) ^1-3-2-6-0

1156. What things are floods? The four floods … these things are separated from floods and floodable. ^1-1156

##### Yokes Group ^1-3-2-7-0

1157. What things are yokes? The four yokes … these things are separated from yokes and yokeable. ^1-1157

##### Hindrances Group ^1-3-2-8-0

1158. What things are hindrances? The six hindrances — hindrance of sense-desire, hindrance of ill will, hindrance of sloth and torpor, hindrance of restlessness and remorse, hindrance of doubt, hindrance of ignorance. ^1-1158

1159. What is the hindrance of sense-desire? Sense-desire in sense pleasures, lust for sense pleasures, delight in sense pleasures, thirst for sense pleasures, fondness for sense pleasures, flooding with sense pleasures, intoxication with sense pleasures, clinging to sense pleasures — this is called the hindrance of sense-desire. ^1-1159

1160. What is the hindrance of ill will? “He did me wrong” — anger arises … discontent of mind — this is called the hindrance of ill will. ^1-1160

1161. What is the hindrance of sloth and torpor? There is sloth; there is torpor. ^1-1161

1162. What is sloth? Unworkability and inability to work of the mind — sinking, settling, being sunken, settling, state of being settled, sloth, being sluggish, state of being sluggish of the mind — this is called sloth. ^1-1162

1163. What is torpor? Unworkability and inability to work of the body — bending down, bending over, inward constriction, torpor, sleep, drowsiness, sleeping, state of sleeping — this is called torpor. So this sloth and this torpor — this is called the hindrance of sloth and torpor. ^1-1163

1164. What is the hindrance of restlessness and remorse? There is restlessness; there is remorse. ^1-1164

1165. What is restlessness? Unsettledness, non-quieting, scattering, wandering state of the mind — this is called restlessness. ^1-1165

1166. What is remorse? Perceiving the improper as proper, perceiving the proper as improper, perceiving what is not wrong as wrong, perceiving what is wrong as not wrong. Whatever remorse of this kind: remorse, being remorseful, state of being remorseful, regret of mind, confusion of mind — this is called remorse. So this restlessness and this remorse — this is called the hindrance of restlessness and remorse. ^1-1166

1167. What is the hindrance of doubt? Doubt and wavering about the teacher … confusion of mind — this is called the hindrance of doubt. ^1-1167

1168. What is the hindrance of ignorance? Not knowing suffering … delusion, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called the hindrance of ignorance.
These things are hindrances. ^1-1168

1169. What things are not hindrances? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not hindrances. ^1-1169

1170. What things are hinderable? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are hinderable. ^1-1170

1171. What things are not hinderable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not hinderable. ^1-1171

1172. What things are united with hindrances? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with hindrances. ^1-1172

1173. What things are separated from hindrances? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from hindrances. ^1-1173

1174. What things are both hindrances and hinderable? Those very hindrances are both hindrances and hinderable. ^1-1174

1175. What things are hinderable but are not hindrances? Things that are hinderable, except those things — the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are hinderable but are not hindrances. ^1-1175

1176. What things are both hindrances and united with hindrances? The hindrance of sense-desire is both hindrance and united with hindrance with the hindrance of ignorance … the hindrance of restlessness is both hindrance and united with hindrance with the hindrance of ignorance, and the hindrance of ignorance is both hindrance and united with hindrance with the hindrance of restlessness — these things are both hindrances and united with hindrances. ^1-1176

1177. What things are united with hindrances but are not hindrances? Things united with those things, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with hindrances but are not hindrances. ^1-1177

1178. What things are separated from hindrances and hinderable? Things separated from those things that are hinderable — things good and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are separated from hindrances and hinderable. ^1-1178

1179. What things are separated from hindrances and not hinderable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from hindrances and not hinderable. ^1-1179

##### Clinging Group (views) ^1-3-2-9-0

1180. What things are clingings? Clinging through views. ^1-1180

1181. What is clinging through views? “The world is eternal,” or “the world is not eternal,” … “the Realized One neither exists nor does not exist after death”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called clinging through views. All wrong view is clinging through views.
These things are clingings. ^1-1181

1182. What things are not clingings? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not clingings. ^1-1182

1183. What things are clingable? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are clingable. ^1-1183

1184. What things are not clingable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not clingable. ^1-1184

1185. What things are united with clingings? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with clingings. ^1-1185

1186. What things are separated from clingings? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from clingings. ^1-1186

1187. What things are both clingings and clingable? That very clinging is both clinging and clingable. ^1-1187

1188. What things are clingable but are not clingings? Things that are clingable, except those things — the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are clingable but are not clingings. ^1-1188

1189. What things are separated from clingings and clingable? Things separated from those things that are clingable — things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are separated from clingings and clingable. ^1-1189

1190. What things are separated from clingings and not clingable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from clingings and not clingable. ^1-1190

##### Large Interior Duals ^1-3-2-10-0

1191. What things have an object? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, aggregate of consciousness — these things have an object. ^1-1191

1192. What things have no object? All form, and the unconditioned element — these things have no object. ^1-1192

1193. What things are mind? Eye-consciousness, ear-consciousness, nose-consciousness, tongue-consciousness, body-consciousness, mind element, mind-consciousness element — these things are mind. ^1-1193

1194. What things are not mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not mind. ^1-1194

1195. What things are mental factors? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions — these things are mental factors. ^1-1195

1196. What things are not mental factors? Mind, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not mental factors. ^1-1196

1197. What things are united with mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions — these things are united with mind. ^1-1197

1198. What things are separated from mind? All form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from mind. Mind should not be said to be either united with mind or separated from mind. ^1-1198

1199. What things are mixed with mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions — these things are mixed with mind. ^1-1199

1200. What things are not mixed with mind? All form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not mixed with mind. Mind should not be said to be either mixed with mind or not mixed with mind. ^1-1200

1201. What things arise from mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions; bodily communication, verbal communication; and whatever other form there may be born of mind, with mind as cause, arising from mind — visible-form sense base, sound sense base, smell sense base, taste sense base, touch sense base, space element, water element, lightness of form, softness of form, readiness of form, growth of form, continuity of form, physical food — these things arise from mind. ^1-1201

1202. What things do not arise from mind? Mind, the remaining form, and the unconditioned element — these things do not arise from mind. ^1-1202

1203. What things accompany mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, bodily communication, verbal communication — these things accompany mind. ^1-1203

1204. What things do not accompany mind? Mind, the remaining form, and the unconditioned element — these things do not accompany mind. ^1-1204

1205. What things follow mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, bodily communication, verbal communication — these things follow mind. ^1-1205

1206. What things do not follow mind? Mind, the remaining form, and the unconditioned element — these things do not follow mind. ^1-1206

1207. What things are mixed with mind and arise from mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions — these things are mixed with mind and arise from mind. ^1-1207

1208. What things are not mixed with mind and do not arise from mind? Mind, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not mixed with mind and do not arise from mind. ^1-1208

1209. What things are mixed with mind, arise from mind, and accompany mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions — these things are mixed with mind, arise from mind, and accompany mind. ^1-1209

1210. What things are not mixed with mind, do not arise from mind, and do not accompany mind? Mind, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not mixed with mind, do not arise from mind, and do not accompany mind. ^1-1210

1211. What things are mixed with mind, arise from mind, and follow mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions — these things are mixed with mind, arise from mind, and follow mind. ^1-1211

1212. What things are not mixed with mind, do not arise from mind, and do not follow mind? Mind, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not mixed with mind, do not arise from mind, and do not follow mind. ^1-1212

1213. What things are internal? Eye sense base … mind sense base — these things are internal. ^1-1213

1214. What things are external? Visible-form sense base … mental-object sense base — these things are external. ^1-1214

1215. What things are derived? Eye sense base … physical food — these things are derived. ^1-1215

1216. What things are not derived? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, aggregate of consciousness, the four great elements, and the unconditioned element — these things are not derived. ^1-1216

1217. What things are taken up? Results of things good and bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; and whatever form is made by action — these things are taken up. ^1-1217

1218. What things are not taken up? Things good and bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; things that are functional — neither good nor bad for the mind nor results of action; whatever form is not made by action; what is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not taken up. ^1-1218

##### Clinging Group ^1-3-2-11-0

1219. What things are clingings? The four clingings — clinging to sense pleasures, clinging to views, clinging to virtue and practice, clinging to personality belief. ^1-1219

1220. What is clinging to sense pleasures? Sense-desire in sense pleasures, lust for sense pleasures, delight in sense pleasures, thirst for sense pleasures, fondness for sense pleasures, flooding with sense pleasures, intoxication with sense pleasures, clinging to sense pleasures — this is called clinging to sense pleasures. ^1-1220

1221. What is clinging to views? “There is no giving, no sacrifice, no offering, no fruit or result of good and bad actions, there is no this world, no other world, no mother, no father, no beings who appear spontaneously, there are in the world no recluses and brahmins who have gone rightly, who go rightly, who by realizing for themselves with direct knowledge proclaim this world and the other world”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called clinging to views. Except clinging to virtue and practice and clinging to personality belief, all wrong view is clinging to views. ^1-1221

1222. What is clinging to virtue and practice? The view of recluses and brahmins outside this teaching that “purity comes from virtue, purity comes from practice, purity comes from virtue and practice”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called clinging to virtue and practice. ^1-1222

1223. What is clinging to personality belief? Here an untaught ordinary person who has not seen the noble ones … regards form as the self … consciousness as the self, or the self as having consciousness, or consciousness as in the self, or the self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called clinging to personality belief.
These things are clingings. ^1-1223

1224. What things are not clingings? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not clingings. ^1-1224

1225. What things are clingable? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are clingable. ^1-1225

1226. What things are not clingable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not clingable. ^1-1226

1227. What things are united with clingings? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with clingings. ^1-1227

1228. What things are separated from clingings? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from clingings. ^1-1228

1229. What things are both clingings and clingable? Those very clingings are both clingings and clingable. ^1-1229

1230. What things are clingable but are not clingings? Things that are clingable, except those things — the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are clingable but are not clingings. ^1-1230

1231. What things are both clingings and united with clingings? Clinging to views is both clinging and united with clinging with clinging to sense pleasures; clinging to sense pleasures is both clinging and united with clinging with clinging to views; clinging to virtue and practice is both clinging and united with clinging with clinging to sense pleasures; clinging to sense pleasures is both clinging and united with clinging with clinging to virtue and practice; clinging to personality belief is both clinging and united with clinging with clinging to sense pleasures; clinging to sense pleasures is both clinging and united with clinging with clinging to personality belief — these things are both clingings and united with clingings. ^1-1231

1232. What things are united with clingings but are not clingings? Things united with those things, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with clingings but are not clingings. ^1-1232

1233. What things are separated from clingings and clingable? Things separated from those things that are clingable — things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are separated from clingings and clingable. ^1-1233

1234. What things are separated from clingings and not clingable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from clingings and not clingable.
Second Recitation Round in the Summary Section. ^1-1234

##### Defilements Group ^1-3-2-12-0

1235. What things are defilements? The ten bases of defilement — greed, hatred, delusion, pride, view, doubt, sloth, restlessness, without conscience, without worry about wrong action. ^1-1235

1236. What is greed? Lust, being lustful … coveting, greed, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called greed. ^1-1236

1237. What is hatred? “He did me wrong” — anger arises … discontent of mind — this is called hatred. ^1-1237

1238. What is delusion? Not knowing suffering … delusion, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called delusion. ^1-1238

1239. What is pride? “I am superior” pride, “I am equal” pride, “I am inferior” pride; whatever pride of this kind: conceiving, state of conceiving, uplift, upliftedness, banner, pretension, desire for recognition in the mind — this is called pride. ^1-1239

1240. What is view? “The world is eternal,” or “the world is not eternal,” … “the Realized One neither exists nor does not exist after death”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called view. All wrong view is view. ^1-1240

1241. What is doubt? Doubt and wavering about the teacher … confusion of mind — this is called doubt. ^1-1241

1242. What is sloth? Unworkability and inability to work of the mind — sinking, settling, being sunken, settling, state of being settled, sloth, being sluggish, state of being sluggish of the mind — this is called sloth. ^1-1242

1243. What is restlessness? Unsettledness, non-quieting, scattering, wandering state of the mind — this is called restlessness. ^1-1243

1244. What is without conscience? Not feeling conscience when one should, not feeling conscience about attaining things bad for the mind — this is called without conscience. ^1-1244

1245. What is without worry about wrong action? Not worrying when one should, not worrying about attaining things bad for the mind — this is called without worry about wrong action.
These things are defilements. ^1-1245

1246. What things are not defilements? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not defilements. ^1-1246

1247. What things are defilementable? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are defilementable. ^1-1247

1248. What things are not defilementable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not defilementable. ^1-1248

1249. What things are stained? The three roots of what is bad for the mind — greed, hatred, delusion; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are stained. ^1-1249

1250. What things are unstained? Things good and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are unstained. ^1-1250

1251. What things are united with defilements? Things united with those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with defilements. ^1-1251

1252. What things are separated from defilements? Things separated from those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from defilements. ^1-1252

1253. What things are both defilements and defilementable? Those very defilements are both defilements and defilementable. ^1-1253

1254. What things are defilementable but are not defilements? Things that are defilementable, except those things — the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are defilementable but are not defilements. ^1-1254

1255. What things are both defilements and stained? Those very defilements are both defilements and stained. ^1-1255

1256. What things are stained but are not defilements? Things that are stained, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are stained but are not defilements. ^1-1256

1257. What things are both defilements and united with defilements? Greed is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with greed; hatred is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with hatred; pride is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with pride; view is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with view; doubt is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with doubt; sloth is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with sloth; restlessness is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with restlessness; without conscience is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with without conscience; without worry about wrong action is both defilement and united with defilement with delusion; delusion is both defilement and united with defilement with without worry about wrong action; greed is both defilement and united with defilement with restlessness, and restlessness with greed; hatred with restlessness, and restlessness with hatred; delusion with restlessness, and restlessness with delusion; pride with restlessness, and restlessness with pride; view with restlessness, and restlessness with view; doubt with restlessness, and restlessness with doubt; sloth with restlessness, and restlessness with sloth; without conscience with restlessness, and restlessness with without conscience; without worry about wrong action with restlessness, and restlessness with without worry about wrong action; greed with without conscience, and without conscience with greed; hatred with without conscience, and without conscience with hatred; delusion with without conscience, and without conscience with delusion; pride with without conscience, and without conscience with pride; view with without conscience, and without conscience with view; doubt with without conscience, and without conscience with doubt; sloth with without conscience, and without conscience with sloth; restlessness with without conscience, and without conscience with restlessness; without worry about wrong action with without conscience, and without conscience with without worry about wrong action; greed with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with greed; hatred with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with hatred; delusion with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with delusion; pride with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with pride; view with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with view; doubt with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with doubt; sloth with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with sloth; restlessness with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with restlessness; without conscience with without worry about wrong action, and without worry about wrong action with without conscience — these things are both defilements and united with defilements. ^1-1257

1258. What things are united with defilements but are not defilements? Things united with those things, except those things — the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness — these things are united with defilements but are not defilements. ^1-1258

1259. What things are separated from defilements and defilementable? Things separated from those things that are defilementable — things good and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are separated from defilements and defilementable. ^1-1259

1260. What things are separated from defilements and not defilementable? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are separated from defilements and not defilementable. ^1-1260

##### Rear Dual Summary ^1-3-2-13-0

1261. What things are to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters — personality view, doubt, misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1261

1262. What is personality view? Here an untaught ordinary person … regards form as the self … consciousness as the self, or the self as having consciousness, or consciousness as in the self, or the self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called personality view. ^1-1262

1263. What is doubt? Doubt and wavering about the teacher … confusion of mind — this is called doubt. ^1-1263

1264. What is misbelief in rites and vows? The view of recluses and brahmins outside this teaching that “purity comes from virtue, purity comes from practice, purity comes from virtue and practice”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called misbelief in rites and vows. These three fetters; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1264

1265. What things are not to be abandoned by seeing? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1265

1266. What things are to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hatred, and delusion; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are to be abandoned by development. ^1-1266

1267. What things are not to be abandoned by development? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not to be abandoned by development. ^1-1267

1268. What things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters — personality view, doubt, misbelief in rites and vows. ^1-1268

1269. What is personality view? Here an untaught ordinary person who has not seen the noble ones, is unskilled and untrained in the noble teaching, has not seen good people, is unskilled and untrained in the good-person teaching, regards form as the self … consciousness as the self, or the self as having consciousness, or consciousness as in the self, or the self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called personality view. ^1-1269

1270. What is doubt? Doubt and wavering about the teacher … confusion of mind — this is called doubt. ^1-1270

1271. What is misbelief in rites and vows? The view of recluses and brahmins outside this teaching that “purity comes from virtue, purity comes from practice, purity comes from virtue and practice”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called misbelief in rites and vows. These three fetters; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. The three fetters — personality view, doubt, misbelief in rites and vows — these things are to be abandoned by seeing. Greed, hatred, and delusion of the same class — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. Defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1271

1272. What things are not causes of things to be abandoned by seeing? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1272

1273. What things are causes of things to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hatred, and delusion — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by development. Defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by development. ^1-1273

1274. What things are not causes of things to be abandoned by development? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not causes of things to be abandoned by development. ^1-1274

1275. What things have thinking? In the level with thinking, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except thinking itself; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things have thinking. ^1-1275

1276. What things have no thinking? In the level without thinking, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; thinking itself, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things have no thinking. ^1-1276

1277. What things have examining? In the level with examining, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except examining itself; the aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things have examining. ^1-1277

1278. What things have no examining? In the level without examining, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; examining itself, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things have no examining. ^1-1278

1279. What things have a feeling of joy? In the level with a feeling of joy, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except the feeling of joy itself; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things have a feeling of joy. ^1-1279

1280. What things have no feeling of joy? In the level without a feeling of joy, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; the feeling of joy itself, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things have no feeling of joy. ^1-1280

1281. What things are accompanied by a feeling of joy? In the level with a feeling of joy, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except the feeling of joy itself; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are accompanied by a feeling of joy. ^1-1281

1282. What things are not accompanied by a feeling of joy? In the level not accompanied by a feeling of joy, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; the feeling of joy itself, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not accompanied by a feeling of joy. ^1-1282

1283. What things are accompanied by happiness? In the pleasant level, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included, except happiness itself; the aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are accompanied by happiness. ^1-1283

1284. What things are not accompanied by happiness? In the level not accompanied by happiness, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; happiness itself, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not accompanied by happiness. ^1-1284

1285. What things are accompanied by calm balance? In the level with calm balance, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included, except calm balance itself; the aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, and aggregate of consciousness that accompany them — these things are accompanied by calm balance. ^1-1285

1286. What things are not accompanied by calm balance? In the level not accompanied by calm balance, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; calm balance itself, all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not accompanied by calm balance. ^1-1286

1287. What things belong to the sense-desire level? Making the Avīci hell the lower limit and the Paranimmita-Vasavattī devas the upper limit, whatever aggregates, elements, and sense bases belong within this range here — form, feeling, perception, habits and intentions, consciousness — these things belong to the sense-desire level. ^1-1287

1288. What things do not belong to the sense-desire level? Things belonging to the form level, formless level, and what is not included — these things do not belong to the sense-desire level. ^1-1288

1289. What things belong to the form level? Making the Brahma world the lower limit and the Akaniṭṭha devas the upper limit, whatever mind-and-mental-factor things belong within this range here — of one who has attained absorption, or who has been reborn, or who dwells in happiness visible in this very life — these things belong to the form level. ^1-1289

1290. What things do not belong to the form level? Things belonging to the sense-desire level, formless level, and what is not included — these things do not belong to the form level. ^1-1290

1291. What things belong to the formless level? Making the devas who enjoy the base of infinite space the lower limit and the devas who enjoy the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception the upper limit, whatever mind-and-mental-factor things belong within this range here — of one who has attained absorption, or who has been reborn, or who dwells in happiness visible in this very life — these things belong to the formless level. ^1-1291

1292. What things do not belong to the formless level? Things belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and what is not included — these things do not belong to the formless level. ^1-1292

1293. What things are included? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things are included. ^1-1293

1294. What things are not included? The paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things are not included. ^1-1294

1295. What things lead out? The four paths that are not included — these things lead out. ^1-1295

1296. What things do not lead out? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things do not lead out. ^1-1296

1297. What things are fixed? The five actions with immediate result, fixed wrong view, and the four paths that are not included — these things are fixed. ^1-1297

1298. What things are not fixed? Except those things, the remaining things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not fixed. ^1-1298

1299. What things have a lower? Things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind that have mental stains, belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of form … aggregate of consciousness — these things have a lower. ^1-1299

1300. What things have no lower? What is not included, the paths, the path results, and the unconditioned element — these things have no lower. ^1-1300

1301. What things are a refuge for bad states? The three roots of what is bad for the mind — greed, hatred, delusion; defilements of the same class; the aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness that accompany them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action that arise from them — these things are a refuge for bad states. ^1-1301

1302. What things are not a refuge for bad states? Things good and neither good nor bad for the mind belonging to the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and what is not included; aggregate of feeling … aggregate of consciousness; all form, and the unconditioned element — these things are not a refuge for bad states.
Abhidhamma Dual Summary. ^1-1302

#### Sutta-Style Dual Summary ^1-3-3-0

1303. What things share in direct knowledge? Things united with direct knowledge — these things share in direct knowledge. ^1-1303

1304. What things share in not-knowing? Things united with not-knowing — these things share in not-knowing. ^1-1304

1305. What things are like a lightning flash? Wisdom in the three lower noble paths — these things are like a lightning flash. ^1-1305

1306. What things are like a diamond? Wisdom in the highest path of arahantship — these things are like a diamond. ^1-1306

1307. What things are fools? Without conscience and without worry about wrong action — these things are fools. All things bad for the mind are fools. ^1-1307

1308. What things are wise ones? Conscience and worry about wrong action — these things are wise ones. All things good for the mind are wise ones. ^1-1308

1309. What things are dark? Without conscience and without worry about wrong action — these things are dark. All things bad for the mind are dark. ^1-1309

1310. What things are bright? Conscience and worry about wrong action — these things are bright? All things good for the mind are bright. ^1-1310

1311. What things cause remorse? Bodily misconduct, verbal misconduct, mental misconduct — these things cause remorse. All things bad for the mind cause remorse. ^1-1311

1312. What things cause no remorse? Bodily good conduct, verbal good conduct, mental good conduct — these things cause no remorse. All things good for the mind cause no remorse. ^1-1312

1313. What things are designations? For those various things — counting, common label, designation, expression, name, naming, naming activity, term, phrase, speech — these things are designations. All things are paths of designation. ^1-1313

1314. What things are expressions? For those various things — counting, common label, designation, expression, name, naming, naming activity, term, phrase, speech — these things are expressions. All things are paths of expression. ^1-1314

1315. What things are designations? For those various things — counting, common label, designation, expression, name, naming, naming activity, term, phrase, speech — these things are designations. All things are paths of designation. ^1-1315

1316. What therein is name? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of habits and intentions, aggregate of consciousness, and the unconditioned element — this is called name. ^1-1316

1317. What therein is form? The four great elements and form derived from the four great elements — this is called form. ^1-1317

1318. What therein is not-knowing? Not knowing suffering … delusion, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called not-knowing. ^1-1318

1319. What therein is thirst for existence? Desire for existence in existences … flooding with existence — this is called thirst for existence. ^1-1319

1320. What therein is existence view? “There will be a self and a world”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called existence view. ^1-1320

1321. What therein is non-existence view? “There will not be a self and a world”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called non-existence view. ^1-1321

1322. What therein is eternal view? “The self and the world are eternal”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called eternal view. ^1-1322

1323. What therein is annihilation view? “The self and the world will be cut off”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called annihilation view. ^1-1323

1324. What therein is finite view? “The self and the world are finite”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called finite view. ^1-1324

1325. What therein is infinite view? “The self and the world are infinite”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called infinite view. ^1-1325

1326. What therein is view about the past? Whatever view that arises concerning the past … upside-down grasping — this is called view about the past. ^1-1326

1327. What therein is view about the future? Whatever view that arises concerning the future … upside-down grasping — this is called view about the future. ^1-1327

1328. What therein is without conscience? Not feeling conscience when one should, not feeling conscience about attaining things bad for the mind — this is called without conscience. ^1-1328

1329. What therein is without worry about wrong action? Not worrying when one should, not worrying about attaining things bad for the mind — this is called without worry about wrong action. ^1-1329

1330. What therein is conscience? Feeling conscience when one should, feeling conscience about attaining things bad for the mind — this is called conscience. ^1-1330

1331. What therein is worry about wrong action? Worrying when one should, worrying about attaining things bad for the mind — this is called worry about wrong action. ^1-1331

1332. What therein is being hard to admonish? When spoken to about what is proper, being hard to admonish, hardness to admonish, state of being hard to admonish, taking up what is contrary, state of being contrary, disrespect, state of disrespect, lack of respect, lack of deference — this is called being hard to admonish. ^1-1332

1333. What therein is bad friendship? Those persons who are without faith, without virtue, with little learning, stingy, with little wisdom — associating with them, following them, going along with them, being intimate with them, being devoted to them, being attached to them, being in their company — this is called bad friendship. ^1-1333

1334. What therein is being easy to admonish? When spoken to about what is proper, being easy to admonish, easiness to admonish, state of being easy to admonish, not taking up what is contrary, state of not being contrary, respect, state of respect, having respect, having deference — this is called being easy to admonish. ^1-1334

1335. What therein is good friendship? Those persons who have faith, virtue, much learning, generosity, wisdom — associating with them, following them, going along with them, being intimate with them, being devoted to them, being attached to them, being in their company — this is called good friendship. ^1-1335

1336. What therein is skill regarding offenses? The five classes of offenses, the seven classes of offenses. Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view regarding skill in those offenses — this is called skill regarding offenses. ^1-1336

1337. What therein is skill in rising from offenses? Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view regarding skill in rising from those offenses — this is called skill in rising from offenses. ^1-1337

1338. What therein is skill regarding attainments? There is attainment with thinking and examining; there is attainment with only examining; there is attainment without thinking and without examining. Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view regarding skill in those attainments — this is called skill regarding attainments. ^1-1338

1339. What therein is skill in rising from attainments? Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view regarding skill in rising from those attainments — this is called skill in rising from attainments. ^1-1339

1340. What therein is skill regarding elements? The eighteen elements — eye element, visible-form element, eye-consciousness element … mind element, mental-object element, mind-consciousness element. Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view regarding skill in those elements — this is called skill regarding elements. ^1-1340

1341. What therein is skill in attention? Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view regarding skill in attention to those elements — this is called skill in attention. ^1-1341

1342. What therein is skill regarding sense bases? The twelve sense bases — eye sense base, visible-form sense base … mind sense base, mental-object sense base. Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view regarding skill in those sense bases — this is called skill regarding sense bases. ^1-1342

1343. What therein is skill in dependent origination? From not-knowing come habits and intentions; from habits and intentions consciousness; from consciousness name-and-form; from name-and-form the six sense bases; from the six sense bases contact; from contact feeling; from feeling thirst; from thirst clinging; from clinging becoming; from becoming birth; from birth aging-and-death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, grief, and despair come to be; thus there is the arising of this whole mass of suffering. Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view therein — this is called skill in dependent origination. ^1-1343

1344. What therein is skill regarding what is possible? For whatever things, of whatever things, there are causes and supporting conditions for arising — whatever wisdom, knowing … right view therein — this is called skill regarding what is possible. ^1-1344

1345. What therein is skill regarding what is impossible? For whatever things, of whatever things, there are no causes and no supporting conditions for arising — whatever wisdom, knowing … right view therein — this is called skill regarding what is impossible. ^1-1345

1346. What therein is uprightness? Whatever uprightness of this kind — straightness, not crookedness, not bentness — this is called uprightness. ^1-1346

1347. What therein is gentleness? Whatever softness, gentleness, not harshness, not hardness, lowliness of mind — this is called gentleness. ^1-1347

1348. What therein is patience? Whatever patience, enduring, tolerating, not fierceness, not cruelty, contentment of mind — this is called patience. ^1-1348

1349. What therein is mildness? Bodily non-transgression, verbal non-transgression, bodily-and-verbal non-transgression — this is called mildness. All restraint of virtue is mildness. ^1-1349

1350. What therein is courtesy? Abandoning speech that is rough, harsh, hurtful to others, provoking anger, not leading to calm — and speaking speech that is smooth, pleasing to the ear, affectionate, going to the heart, polite, pleasing and agreeable to many people; whatever courtesy of speech, gentleness of speech, non-harshness of speech therein — this is called courtesy. ^1-1350

1351. What therein is hospitality? Two kinds of hospitality — hospitality with material things and hospitality with the teaching. Here someone is hospitable with hospitality through material things or through the teaching — this is called hospitality. ^1-1351

1352. What therein is lack of guarding the doors of the senses? Here someone, having seen a form with the eye, grasps at signs and grasps at details. Because the eye faculty would be unrestrained for him, bad things bad for the mind — covetousness and discontent — might flow in, he does not practice restraint, does not protect the eye faculty, does not undertake restraint of the eye faculty. Having heard a sound with the ear … smelled a smell with the nose … tasted a taste with the tongue … felt a touch with the body … cognized a mental object with the mind, he grasps at signs and grasps at details. Because the mind faculty would be unrestrained for him, bad things bad for the mind — covetousness and discontent — might flow in, he does not practice restraint, does not protect the mind faculty, does not undertake restraint of the mind faculty. Whatever lack of guarding, lack of protecting, lack of keeping, lack of restraint of these six faculties — this is called lack of guarding the doors of the senses. ^1-1352

1353. What therein is lack of moderation in eating? Here someone eats food without reflection and unwisely — for fun, for intoxication, for beautification, for decoration. Whatever lack of satisfaction, lack of moderation, lack of reflection in eating food therein — this is called lack of moderation in eating. ^1-1353

1354. What therein is guarding the doors of the senses? Here someone, having seen a form with the eye, does not grasp at signs and does not grasp at details. Because the eye faculty would be unrestrained for him, bad things bad for the mind — covetousness and discontent — might flow in, he practices restraint, protects the eye faculty, undertakes restraint of the eye faculty. Having heard a sound with the ear … smelled a smell with the nose … tasted a taste with the tongue … felt a touch with the body … cognized a mental object with the mind, he does not grasp at signs and does not grasp at details. Because the mind faculty would be unrestrained for him, bad things bad for the mind — covetousness and discontent — might flow in, he practices restraint, protects the mind faculty, undertakes restraint of the mind faculty. Whatever guarding, protecting, keeping, restraint of these six faculties — this is called guarding the doors of the senses. ^1-1354

1355. What therein is moderation in eating? Here someone eats food with reflection and wisely — not for fun, not for intoxication, not for beautification, not for decoration, but only for the maintenance and continuance of this body, for ending harm, for supporting the holy life, thinking: “Thus I shall put away old feeling and not produce new feeling, and there will be for me blameless living and comfort.” Whatever satisfaction, moderation, reflection in eating food therein — this is called moderation in eating. ^1-1355

1356. What therein is loss of mindfulness? Whatever forgetfulness, non-recollection, non-mindfulness, forgetfulness, lack of taking up, lack of bearing, slipping away, scattering of the mind — this is called loss of mindfulness. ^1-1356

1357. What therein is lack of clear comprehension? Not knowing suffering … delusion, a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called lack of clear comprehension. ^1-1357

1358. What therein is paying close attention? Whatever mindfulness, recollection, bearing in mind, mindfulness, taking up, bearing, not slipping away, not scattering of the mind, power of paying close attention, strength of paying close attention, right paying close attention — this is called paying close attention. ^1-1358

1359. What therein is clear comprehension? Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view — this is called clear comprehension. ^1-1359

1360. What therein is the power of reflection? Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view — this is called the power of reflection. ^1-1360

1361. What therein is the power of development? Whatever repeated practice, development, making much of things good for the mind — this is called the power of development. The seven factors of awakening are also the power of development. ^1-1361

1362. What therein is calm? Whatever stability of mind … right calm focus — this is called calm. ^1-1362

1363. What therein is insight? Whatever wisdom, knowing … right view — this is called insight. ^1-1363

1364. What therein is the mark of calm? Whatever stability of mind … right calm focus — this is called the mark of calm. ^1-1364

1365. What therein is the mark of exertion? Whatever mental energy, arousing of energy … right effort — this is called the mark of exertion. ^1-1365

1366. What therein is exertion? Whatever mental energy, arousing of energy … right effort — this is called exertion. ^1-1366

1367. What therein is non-distraction? Whatever stability of mind … right calm focus — this is called non-distraction. ^1-1367

1368. What therein is failure of virtue? Bodily transgression, verbal transgression, bodily-and-verbal transgression — this is called failure of virtue. All lack of virtue is failure of virtue. ^1-1368

1369. What therein is failure of view? “There is no giving, no sacrifice, no offering, no fruit or result of good and bad actions, there is no this world, no other world, no mother, no father, no beings who appear spontaneously, there are in the world no recluses and brahmins who have gone rightly, who go rightly, who by realizing for themselves with direct knowledge proclaim this world and the other world”; whatever view of this kind … upside-down grasping — this is called failure of view. All wrong view is failure of view. ^1-1369

1370. What therein is accomplishment in virtue? Bodily non-transgression, verbal non-transgression, bodily-and-verbal non-transgression — this is called accomplishment in virtue. All restraint of virtue is accomplishment in virtue. ^1-1370

1371. What therein is accomplishment in view? “There is giving, there is sacrifice, there is offering, there is fruit and result of good and bad actions, there is this world, there is the other world, there is mother, there is father, there are beings who appear spontaneously, there are in the world recluses and brahmins who have gone rightly, who go rightly, who by realizing for themselves with direct knowledge proclaim this world and the other world”; whatever wisdom, knowing … right view of this kind — this is called accomplishment in view. All right view is accomplishment in view. ^1-1371

1372. What therein is purity of virtue? Bodily non-transgression, verbal non-transgression, bodily-and-verbal non-transgression — this is called purity of virtue. All restraint of virtue is purity of virtue. ^1-1372

1373. What therein is purity of view? Knowledge of ownership of action, knowledge conforming to truth, knowledge of one possessed of the path, knowledge of one possessed of the fruit. ^1-1373

1374. And purity of view is — whatever wisdom, knowing … right view. ^1-1374

1375. And effort in accord with view is — whatever mental energy, arousing of energy … right effort. ^1-1375

1376. And urgency is — fear of birth, fear of aging, fear of sickness, fear of death. And what stirs urgency is — birth, aging, sickness, death. ^1-1376

1377. And right effort for one stirred is — here a monk generates desire, strives, initiates energy, exerts the mind, makes effort for the non-arising of unarisen bad things bad for the mind; generates desire … for the abandoning of arisen bad things bad for the mind; generates desire … for the arising of unarisen things good for the mind; generates desire … for the maintenance, non-confusion, increase, fullness, development, and completion of arisen things good for the mind. ^1-1377

1378. And discontent with things good for the mind is — desire for more through development of things good for the mind. ^1-1378

1379. And non-turning back from effort is — careful doing, persistent doing, not-slipping doing, not-lazy conduct, not-dropping desire, not-dropping energy, repeated practice, development, making much of things good for the mind through development of things good for the mind. ^1-1379

1380. And direct knowledge is — the three kinds of direct knowledge: knowledge of recollection of past lives is direct knowledge; knowledge of the passing away and rebirth of beings is direct knowledge; knowledge of the destruction of mental stains is direct knowledge. ^1-1380

1381. And liberation is — the two liberations: resolve of mind, and nibbana. ^1-1381

1382. And knowledge at destruction is — knowledge of one possessed of the path. ^1-1382

1383. And knowledge at non-arising is — knowledge of one possessed of the fruit.
The Summary Section is finished. ^1-1383

### Commentary Section ^1-4-0

#### Triple Meaning Summary ^1-4-1-0

1384. What things are good for the mind? Things good for the mind in four levels — these things are good for the mind. ^1-1384

1385. What things are bad for the mind? Twelve unwholesome mind-arisings — these things are bad for the mind. ^1-1385

1386. What things are neither good nor bad for the mind? Result in four levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, form, and nibbana — these things are neither good nor bad for the mind. ^1-1386

1387. What things are united with pleasant feeling? From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings accompanied by happiness; from unwholesome, four; from result and functional of sense-desire-level wholesome, five each; form-level second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional; supramundane second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome and result, except the happiness that arises therein — these things are united with pleasant feeling. ^1-1387

1388. What things are united with painful feeling? Two mind-arisings accompanied by dejection, body-consciousness accompanied by pain, except the pain that arises therein — these things are united with painful feeling. ^1-1388

1389. What things are united with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling? From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings accompanied by calm balance; from unwholesome, six; from result of sense-desire-level wholesome, ten; from result of unwholesome, six; functionally, six; form-level fourth absorption from wholesome, result, and functional; four formless levels from wholesome, result, and functional; supramundane fourth absorption from wholesome and result, except the calm balance that arises therein — these things are united with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling. The three feelings, form, and nibbana — these things should not be said to be united with pleasant feeling, nor united with painful feeling, nor united with feeling. ^1-1389

1390. What things are results? Result in four levels — these things are results. ^1-1390

1391. What things are things that produce result? Wholesome and unwholesome in four levels — these things are things that produce result. ^1-1391

1392. What things are neither results nor things that produce result? Functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, form, and nibbana — these things are neither results nor things that produce result. ^1-1392

1393. What things are taken up and can be clung to? Result in three levels, and whatever form is made by action — these things are taken up and can be clung to. ^1-1393

1394. What things are not taken up and can be clung to? Things good and bad for the mind in three levels, functional things neither good nor bad for the mind in three levels, and whatever form is not made by action — these things are not taken up and can be clung to. ^1-1394

1395. What things are not taken up and cannot be clung to? The four paths that are not included, the four noble-fruition results, and nibbana — these things are not taken up and cannot be clung to. ^1-1395

1396. What things are stained and stain? Twelve unwholesome mind-arisings — these things are stained and stain. ^1-1396

1397. What things are unstained and stain? Wholesome in three levels, result in three levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, and all form — these things are unstained and stain. ^1-1397

1398. What things are unstained and do not stain? The four paths that are not included, the four noble-fruition results, and nibbana — these things are unstained and do not stain. ^1-1398

1399. What things have thinking and examining? Sense-desire-level wholesome, unwholesome, eleven mind-arisings from result of sense-desire-level wholesome, two from result of unwholesome, eleven functionally, form-level first absorption from wholesome, result, and functional, supramundane first absorption from wholesome and result, except thinking and examining that arise therein — these things have thinking and examining. ^1-1399

1400. What things have only examining? In the fivefold method, form-level second absorption from wholesome, result, and functional; in the fivefold method, supramundane second absorption from wholesome and result, except examining that arises therein, and thinking — these things have only examining. ^1-1400

1401. What things have no thinking and no examining? The two sets of five consciousnesses, form-level second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional, four formless levels from wholesome, result, and functional, supramundane second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome and result in the fivefold method at the second absorption, examining that has arisen, form, and nibbana — these things have no thinking and no examining. Examining born together with thinking should not be said to have thinking and examining, nor only examining, nor no thinking and no examining. ^1-1401

1402. What things are accompanied by a feeling of joy? From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings accompanied by happiness; from unwholesome, four; from result of sense-desire-level wholesome, five; functionally, five; form-level first-and-second absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional; supramundane first-and-second absorptions from wholesome and result, except the feeling of joy that arises therein — these things are accompanied by a feeling of joy. ^1-1402

1403. What things are accompanied by happiness? From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings accompanied by happiness; from unwholesome, four; from result of sense-desire-level wholesome, six; functionally, five; form-level second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional; supramundane second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome and result, except happiness that arises therein — these things are accompanied by happiness. ^1-1403

1404. What things are accompanied by calm balance? From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings accompanied by calm balance; from unwholesome, six; from result of sense-desire-level wholesome, ten; from result of unwholesome, six; functionally, six; form-level fourth absorption from wholesome, result, and functional; four formless levels from wholesome, result, and functional; supramundane fourth absorption from wholesome and result, except calm balance that arises therein — these things are accompanied by calm balance. A feeling of joy is not accompanied by a feeling of joy, is accompanied by happiness, is not accompanied by calm balance. Happiness is not accompanied by happiness, may be accompanied by a feeling of joy, is not accompanied by calm balance, and may not be said to be accompanied by a feeling of joy. Two mind-arisings accompanied by dejection, body-consciousness accompanied by pain, the feeling that is calm balance, form, and nibbana — these things should not be said to be accompanied by a feeling of joy, nor accompanied by happiness, nor accompanied by calm balance. ^1-1404

1405. What things are to be abandoned by seeing? Four mind-arisings united with wrong view, mind-arising accompanied by doubt — these things are to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1405

1406. What things are to be abandoned by development? Mind-arising accompanied by restlessness — these things are to be abandoned by development. Four mind-arisings accompanied by greed, separated from wrong view, two mind-arisings accompanied by dejection — these things may be to be abandoned by seeing, may be to be abandoned by development. ^1-1406

1407. What things are not to be abandoned by seeing nor by development? Wholesome in four levels, result in four levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, form, and nibbana — these things are not to be abandoned by seeing nor by development. ^1-1407

1408. What things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing? Four mind-arisings united with wrong view, mind-arising accompanied by doubt, except delusion that arises therein — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1408

1409. What things are causes of things to be abandoned by development? Mind-arising accompanied by restlessness, except delusion that arises therein — these things are causes of things to be abandoned by development. Four mind-arisings accompanied by greed, separated from wrong view, two mind-arisings accompanied by dejection — these things may be causes of things to be abandoned by seeing, may be causes of things to be abandoned by development. ^1-1409

1410. What things are not causes of things to be abandoned by seeing nor by development? Delusion accompanied by doubt, delusion accompanied by restlessness, wholesome in four levels, result in four levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, form, and nibbana — these things are not causes of things to be abandoned by seeing nor by development. ^1-1410

1411. What things lead to increase? Wholesome and unwholesome in three levels — these things lead to increase. ^1-1411

1412. What things lead to decrease? The four paths that are not included — these things lead to decrease. ^1-1412

1413. What things neither lead to increase nor to decrease? Result in four levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, form, and nibbana — these things neither lead to increase nor to decrease. ^1-1413

1414. What things belong to one in training? The four paths that are not included, and the three lower noble-fruition results — these things belong to one in training. ^1-1414

1415. What things belong to one beyond training? The highest noble-fruition result of full awakening — these things belong to one beyond training. ^1-1415

1416. What things neither belong to one in training nor beyond training? Wholesome and unwholesome in three levels, result in three levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, form, and nibbana — these things neither belong to one in training nor beyond training. ^1-1416

1417. What things are small? Sense-desire-level wholesome, unwholesome, all result of sense-desire level, sense-desire-level functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind, and all form — these things are small. ^1-1417

1418. What things are great? Form-level, formless-level, wholesome and neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind — these things are great. ^1-1418

1419. What things are unlimited? The four paths that are not included, the four noble-fruition results, and nibbana — these things are unlimited. ^1-1419

1420. What things have small objects? All result of sense-desire level, functional mind element, functional mind-consciousness element with root-causes accompanied by happiness — these things have small objects. ^1-1420

1421. What things have great objects? Base of infinite consciousness, base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception — these things have great objects. ^1-1421

1422. What things have unlimited objects? The four paths that are not included and the four noble-fruition results — these things have unlimited objects. From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings separated from knowledge; functionally, four mind-arisings separated from knowledge; all unwholesome — these things may have small objects, may have great objects, do not have unlimited objects, and may not be said to have small objects or great objects. From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings united with knowledge; functionally, four mind-arisings united with knowledge; form-level fourth absorption from wholesome and functional; functional mind-consciousness element with root-causes accompanied by calm balance — these things may have small objects, may have great objects, may have unlimited objects, and may not be said to have small objects, great objects, or unlimited objects. Form-level second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional, result of the fourth absorption, base of infinite space, base of nothingness — these things should not be said to have small objects, great objects, or unlimited objects. Form and nibbana have no object. ^1-1422

1423. What things are inferior? Twelve unwholesome mind-arisings — these things are inferior. ^1-1423

1424. What things are middling? Wholesome in three levels, result in three levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, and all form — these things are middling. ^1-1424

1425. What things are excellent? The four paths that are not included, the four noble-fruition results, and nibbana — these things are excellent. ^1-1425

1426. What things have fixed wrongness? Four mind-arisings united with wrong view, two mind-arisings accompanied by dejection — these things may have fixed wrongness, may be not fixed. ^1-1426

1427. What things have fixed rightness? The four paths that are not included — these things have fixed rightness. ^1-1427

1428. What things are not fixed? Four mind-arisings accompanied by greed, separated from wrong view, mind-arising accompanied by doubt, mind-arising accompanied by restlessness, wholesome in three levels, result in four levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, form, and nibbana — these things are not fixed. ^1-1428

1429. What things have the path as object? From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings united with knowledge; functionally, four mind-arisings united with knowledge — these things may have the path as object, are not path-causes; may be dominated by the path, and may not be said to have the path as object or be dominated by the path. The four noble paths do not have the path as object, are path-causes; may be dominated by the path, and may not be said to be dominated by the path. Form-level fourth absorption from wholesome and functional, functional mind-consciousness element with root-causes accompanied by calm balance — these things may have the path as object; are not path-causes, are not dominated by the path; and may not be said to have the path as object. From sense-desire-level wholesome, four mind-arisings separated from knowledge, all unwholesome, all result of sense-desire level, six mind-arisings functionally, form-level second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional, result of the fourth absorption, four formless levels from wholesome, result, and functional, and the four noble-fruition results — these things should not be said to have the path as object, be path-causes, or be dominated by the path. Form and nibbana have no object. ^1-1429

1430. What things have arisen? Result in four levels, and whatever form is made by action — these things may have arisen, may be producers; should not be said to be unarisen. Wholesome and unwholesome in four levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, and whatever form is not made by action — these things may have arisen, may be unarisen; should not be said to be producers. Nibbana should not be said to be arisen, unarisen, or a producer. ^1-1430

1431. Except nibbana, all things may be past, may be future, may be present. Nibbana should not be said to be past, future, or present. ^1-1431

1432. What things have past objects? Base of infinite consciousness, base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception — these things have past objects. ^1-1432

1433. By rule there is no future object. ^1-1433

1434. What things have present objects? The two sets of five consciousnesses, and the three mind elements — these things have present objects. From result of sense-desire-level wholesome, ten mind-arisings; from result of unwholesome, mind-consciousness element accompanied by calm balance; functional mind-consciousness element with root-causes accompanied by happiness — these things may have past objects, may have future objects, may have present objects. Sense-desire-level wholesome, unwholesome, nine mind-arisings functionally, form-level fourth absorption from wholesome and functional — these things may have past objects, may have future objects, may have present objects; and may not be said to have past objects, future objects, or present objects. Form-level second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional, result of the fourth absorption, base of infinite space, base of nothingness, the four paths that are not included, and the four noble-fruition results — these things should not be said to have past objects, future objects, or present objects. Form and nibbana have no object. ^1-1434

1435. Except form not bound to faculties and nibbana, all things may be internal, may be external, may be internal and external. Form not bound to faculties and nibbana are external. ^1-1435

1436. What things have internal objects? Base of infinite consciousness, base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception — these things have internal objects. ^1-1436

1437. What things have external objects? Form-level second-through-fourth absorptions from wholesome, result, and functional, result of the fourth absorption, base of infinite space, the four paths that are not included, and the four noble-fruition results — these things have external objects. Except form, all sense-desire-level things good, bad, and neither good nor bad for the mind, form-level fourth absorption from wholesome and functional — these things may have internal objects, may have external objects, may have internal and external objects. Base of nothingness should not be said to have internal objects, external objects, or internal and external objects. Form and nibbana have no object. ^1-1437

1438. What things can be seen and can be pushed against? Visible-form sense base — these things can be seen and can be pushed against. ^1-1438

1439. What things cannot be seen and can be pushed against? Eye sense base … touch sense base — these things cannot be seen and can be pushed against. ^1-1439

1440. What things cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against? Wholesome and unwholesome in four levels, result in four levels, functional neither-good-nor-bad-for-the-mind in three levels, whatever form cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against that is included in the mental-object sense base, and nibbana — these things cannot be seen and cannot be pushed against.
Triples. ^1-1440

#### Dual Meaning Summary ^1-4-2-0

##### Causes Group ^1-4-2-1-0
